Read file C:\Users\bijim\eek1\src\billing.ts, lines 1-39
1: // The paid user plan in Clerk Billing (Dashboard → Billing → Plans → User Plans)
2: export const PRO_PLAN_SLUG = "pro";
3: 
4: type SubscriptionItem = { status: string; plan: { slug: string } };
5: type Subscription = { subscription_items: SubscriptionItem[] };
6: 
7: // Webhook requests carry no Clerk session, so `has({ plan })` isn't available; the plan is read
8: // from the Backend API by the Clerk user id the chat account is linked to instead
9: export const hasProPlan = async (clerkUserId: string) => {
10:   const res = await fetch(
11:     `https://api.clerk.com/v1/users/${encodeURIComponent(clerkUserId)}/billing/subscription`,
12:     { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${process.env.CLERK_SECRET_KEY}` } }
13:   );
14:   // No subscription at all, e.g. a user created before Billing was enabled
15:   if (res.status === 404) return false;
16:   if (!res.ok) {
17:     throw new Error(`Subscription lookup failed: ${res.status} ${await res.text()}`);
18:   }
19:   const subscription = (await res.json()) as Subscription;
20:   // A canceled Pro item stays `active` until its period ends; upcoming (scheduled) items don't count
21:   return subscription.subscription_items.some(
22:     (item) => item.plan.slug === PRO_PLAN_SLUG && item.status === "active"
23:   );
24: };
25: 
26: // The hosted Account Portal lives next to the Frontend API, whose host is encoded in the
27: // publishable key: `<slug>.clerk.accounts.dev` → `<slug>.accounts.dev` in development,
28: // `clerk.<domain>` → `accounts.<domain>` in production
29: const accountPortalUrl = () => {
30:   const key = process.env.CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY!;
31:   const frontendApi = Buffer.from(key.split("_")[2], "base64").toString().replace(/\$$/, "");
32:   const host = frontendApi.endsWith(".clerk.accounts.dev")
33:     ? frontendApi.replace(/\.clerk\.accounts\.dev$/, ".accounts.dev")
34:     : frontendApi.replace(/^clerk\./, "accounts.");
35:   return `https://${host}`;
36: };
37: 
38: // The Billing tab of the Account Portal's user profile, where users can subscribe
39: export const upgradeUrl = () => `${accountPortalUrl()}/user/billing`;

1: import { timingSafeEqual } from "node:crypto";
2: import * as Sentry from "@sentry/node";
3: import { hasProPlan, upgradeUrl } from "./billing.js";
4: import { claimProWelcome, findChannelLink, markUpgradePrompted } from "./db/channel-links.js";
5: import { insertInbox } from "./db/chat.js";
6: import type { InboxPart, Platform } from "./db/schema.js";
7: import { CHANNELS } from "./inngest/channels.js";
8: import { inngest } from "./inngest/client.js";
9: import { accountLinkRequested, chatMessageReceived } from "./inngest/events.js";
10: import { log } from "./log.js";
11: 
12: // What every channel webhook does once it has verified and parsed a platform's message: only
13: // messages from users linked to a Clerk account on the Pro plan reach the agent. Unlinked users are
14: // sent a sign-in link, linked users without Pro an upgrade link
15: 
16: export const secretMatches = (received: string | undefined, expected: string | undefined) => {
17:   if (!received || !expected) return false;
18:   const a = Buffer.from(received);
19:   const b = Buffer.from(expected);
20:   return a.length === b.length && timingSafeEqual(a, b);
21: };
22: 
23: // Sent once, on the first message after a user who was shown the upgrade prompt is on Pro
24: export const proWelcomeMessage = () =>
25:   "You have upgraded to Pro. You are now free to ask me anything.";
26: 
27: export const upgradeMessage = () =>
28:   [
29:     "The assistant is available on the Pro plan.",
30:     "",
31:     `Upgrade here, then send me a message again: ${upgradeUrl()}`,
32:   ].join("\n");
33: 
34: export type GateResult =
35:   // An account-linking run is (or already was) messaging the user a sign-in link
36:   | { status: "linking" }
37:   // Linked, but not on Pro: the webhook replies with upgradeMessage() its own way
38:   | { status: "upgrade" }
39:   // `welcome` is true for the first message after a prompted user subscribed
40:   | { status: "allowed"; clerkUserId: string; welcome: boolean };
41: 
42: // Throws when the plan check fails, so the webhook can answer with a non-2xx and the platform
43: // redelivers instead of the message being dropped
44: export const gateInbound = async ({
45:   platform,
46:   externalUserId,
47:   chatId,
48: }: {
49:   platform: Platform;
50:   externalUserId: string;
51:   chatId: string;
52: }): Promise<GateResult> => {
53:   const link = await findChannelLink(platform, externalUserId);
54: 
55:   if (!link) {
56:     // The flow is a singleton per user: while one is waiting for the sign-in, this event is skipped
57:     await inngest.send(accountLinkRequested.create({ platform, externalUserId, chatId }));
58:     return { status: "linking" };
59:   }
60: 
61:   // Opaque Clerk id only: attributes this request's logs, errors and spans to the user
62:   Sentry.setUser({ id: link.clerkUserId });
63:   if (!(await hasProPlan(link.clerkUserId))) {
64:     await markUpgradePrompted(platform, externalUserId);
65:     return { status: "upgrade" };
66:   }
67:   return {
68:     status: "allowed",
69:     clerkUserId: link.clerkUserId,
70:     welcome: await claimProWelcome(platform, externalUserId),
71:   };
72: };
73: 
74: // Queues an allowed message for the agent: stored in the inbox first, then announced. Returns false
75: // on failure so the webhook answers non-2xx and the platform redelivers; both steps are safe to
76: // repeat, since the inbox ignores a message it already has and Inngest drops a repeated event id
77: export const enqueueChatMessage = async ({
78:   platform,
79:   externalMessageId,
80:   clerkUserId,
81:   chatId,
82:   parts,
83: }: {
84:   platform: Platform;
85:   externalMessageId: string;
86:   clerkUserId: string;
87:   chatId: string;
88:   parts: InboxPart[];
89: }) => {
90:   try {
91:     const inserted = await insertInbox({
92:       platform,
93:       externalMessageId,
94:       clerkUserId,
95:       chatId,
96:       parts,
97:     });
98:     await inngest.send(
99:       chatMessageReceived.create(
100:         { platform, clerkUserId, chatId },
101:         { id: `${platform}-${externalMessageId}` }
102:       )
103:     );
104:     // Show the platform's "working…" message as soon as the message is queued instead of waiting
105:     // for the run to start. Only a newly queued message shows one, so a redelivery after the reply
106:     // can't leave an orphan behind
107:     if (inserted && CHANNELS[platform].workingMessage) {
108:       // Cosmetic: a platform failure must not make the webhook answer non-2xx
109:       await CHANNELS[platform].setWorking(chatId, true).catch(() => {});
110:     }
111:     return true;
112:   } catch (error) {
113:     Sentry.captureException(error);
114:     // The message and its ids aren't logged: the error class says whether it's the database or Inngest
115:     log.error("Queueing the message failed", {
116:       "channel.platform": platform,
117:       "error.name": error instanceof Error ? error.name : "unknown",
118:     });
119:     return false;
120:   }
121: };

7: // Long enough for one delivery step, short enough that a crashed run is retried quickly

10: export const insertReminder = async (reminder: NewReminder): Promise<Reminder> => {
11:   if (!reminder.dedupeKey) {
12:     const rows = await db.insert(reminders).values(reminder).returning();
13:     return rows[0]!;
14:   }
15: 
16:   const rows = await db
17:     .insert(reminders)
18:     .values(reminder)
19:     .onConflictDoNothing({ target: reminders.dedupeKey })
20:     .returning();
21:   if (rows[0]) return rows[0];
22: 
23:   const existing = await loadReminderByDedupeKey(reminder.dedupeKey);
24:   if (!existing) throw new Error("Reminder insert conflicted but no existing row was found.");
25:   return existing;
26: };

28: export const loadReminder = async (id: string): Promise<Reminder | null> => {
29:   const rows = await db.select().from(reminders).where(eq(reminders.id, id)).limit(1);
30:   return rows[0] ?? null;

61: // Cancels one series. False means it was already canceled, sent, or belongs to someone else
62: export const cancelReminder = async (clerkUserId: string, id: string): Promise<boolean> => {
63:   const rows = await db
64:     .update(reminders)
65:     .set({ status: "canceled", claimedUntil: null })
66:     .where(
67:       and(
68:         eq(reminders.id, id),
69:         eq(reminders.clerkUserId, clerkUserId),
70:         eq(reminders.status, "pending")
71:       )
72:     )
73:     .returning({ id: reminders.id });
74:   return rows.length > 0;
75: };
76: 
77: // Claims one specific occurrence. Matching dueAt means a delayed event from an old occurrence
78: // cannot deliver the new one. The update is atomic, so the event and the hourly sweep can race
79: export const claimReminder = async (
80:   id: string,
81:   scheduledFor: Date
82: ): Promise<Reminder | null> => {
83:   const rows = await db
84:     .update(reminders)
85:     .set({ claimedUntil: sql`now() + ${CLAIM_MINUTES} * interval '1 minute'` })
86:     .where(
87:       and(
88:         eq(reminders.id, id),
89:         eq(reminders.status, "pending"),
90:         eq(reminders.dueAt, scheduledFor),
91:         or(isNull(reminders.claimedUntil), lte(reminders.claimedUntil, sql`now()`))
92:       )
93:     )
94:     .returning();
95:   return rows[0] ?? null;
96: };
97: 
98: // Takes the due reminders for the hourly fallback. The lease is deliberately short: if the run
99: // dies after claiming, the next sweep picks the reminder up again. The conditional update makes
100: // the claim atomic against a delayed event firing at the same time
101: export const claimDueReminders = async (limit: number): Promise<Reminder[]> => {
102:   const due = await db
103:     .select({ id: reminders.id })
104:     .from(reminders)
105:     .where(
106:       and(
107:         eq(reminders.status, "pending"),
108:         lte(reminders.dueAt, sql`now()`),
109:         or(isNull(reminders.claimedUntil), lte(reminders.claimedUntil, sql`now()`))
110:       )
111:     )
112:     .orderBy(asc(reminders.dueAt))
113:     .limit(limit);
114:   if (due.length === 0) return [];
115: 
116:   return db
117:     .update(reminders)
118:     .set({ claimedUntil: sql`now() + ${CLAIM_MINUTES} * interval '1 minute'` })
119:     .where(
120:       and(
121:         inArray(reminders.id, due.map((row) => row.id)),
122:         eq(reminders.status, "pending"),
123:         lte(reminders.dueAt, sql`now()`),
124:         or(isNull(reminders.claimedUntil), lte(reminders.claimedUntil, sql`now()`))
125:       )
126:     )
127:     .returning();
128: };
129: 
130: export const markReminderSent = async (id: string, nextDueAt: Date | null): Promise<void> => {
131:   if (nextDueAt) {
132:     await db
133:       .update(reminders)
134:       .set({
135:         dueAt: nextDueAt,
136:         status: "pending",
137:         attempts: 0,
138:         claimedUntil: null,
139:         lastSentAt: sql`now()`,
140:       })
141:       .where(eq(reminders.id, id));
142:     return;
143:   }
144:   await db
145:     .update(reminders)
146:     .set({ status: "sent", claimedUntil: null, lastSentAt: sql`now()` })
147:     .where(eq(reminders.id, id));
148: };
149: 
150: export const recordReminderFailure = async (

154:   const status = attempts >= MAX_REMINDER_ATTEMPTS ? "failed" : "pending";

156:   return { attempts, status };
157: };

23: // Confidential OAuth app with Device Authorization Grant enabled, created with the Clerk CLI
24: const oauthRequest = (path: string, body: Record<string, string>) =>
25:   fetch(`${process.env.CLERK_OAUTH_ISSUER}${path}`, {
26:     method: "POST",
27:     headers: {
28:       Authorization: `Basic ${btoa(`${process.env.CLERK_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID}:${process.env.CLERK_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET}`)}`,
29:       "Content-Type": "application/x-www-form-urlencoded",
30:     },
31:     body: new URLSearchParams(body),
32:   });

34: const startDeviceAuthorization = async () => {
35:   const res = await oauthRequest("/oauth/device_authorization", {
36:     client_id: process.env.CLERK_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID!,
37:     scope: "openid email",
38:   });
39:   if (!res.ok) {
40:     throw new Error(`Device authorization failed: ${res.status} ${await res.text()}`);
41:   }
42:   return (await res.json()) as DeviceAuthorization;
43: };

45: const pollToken = async (deviceCode: string) => {
46:   const res = await oauthRequest("/oauth/token", {
47:     grant_type: "urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:device_code",
48:     device_code: deviceCode,
49:   });
50:   // Pending, denied and expired requests come back as 400s with an OAuth error code
51:   if (!res.ok && res.status !== 400) {
52:     throw new Error(`Token request failed: ${res.status} ${await res.text()}`);
53:   }
54:   return (await res.json()) as TokenResponse;
55: };
56: 
57: const fetchUserInfo = async (accessToken: string) => {
58:   const res = await fetch(`${process.env.CLERK_OAUTH_ISSUER}/oauth/userinfo`, {
59:     headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}` },
60:   });
61:   if (!res.ok) {
62:     throw new Error(`userinfo failed: ${res.status} ${await res.text()}`);
63:   }
64:   // `sub` is the Clerk user id
65:   return (await res.json()) as { sub: string; email?: string };
66: };

68: type PollResult =
69:   | { status: "pending" }
70:   | { status: "slow_down" }
71:   | { status: "denied" }
72:   | { status: "expired" }
73:   | { status: "linked"; clerkUserId?: string; email?: string };

75: // Links a chat platform user to their Clerk account: sends them a sign-in link and code, waits for
76: // them to approve it in the browser, then records which Clerk user they are
77: export const linkChannelAccount = inngest.createFunction(
78:   {
79:     id: "link-channel-account",
80:     triggers: [accountLinkRequested],
81:     // A retry would send a second code; let the user start over by messaging again instead
82:     retries: 0,
83:     // One flow per unlinked user at a time, so repeated messages while a code is still valid don't
84:     // each start a new one. The lock frees when the run ends, so the next message may start a new
85:     // flow right away
86:     singleton: { key: 'event.data.platform + ":" + event.data.externalUserId', mode: "skip" },
87:     onFailure: async ({ error, event }) => {
88:       await reportFailure(error, { function: "link-channel-account", runId: event.data.run_id });
89:     },
90:   },
91:   async ({ event, step }) => {
92:     const { platform, externalUserId, chatId } = event.data;
93:     const sendMessage = (text: string) => sendChannelText(platform, chatId, text);
94: 
95:     if (await step.run("check-linked", async () => Boolean(await findChannelLink(platform, externalUserId)))) {
96:       return { status: "already-linked" as const };
97:     }
98: 
99:     const device = await step.run("start-device-authorization", startDeviceAuthorization);
100:     log.info("Account linking started", { "channel.platform": platform });
101:     await step.run("send-link", () =>
102:       sendMessage(
103:         [
104:           "Connect your account to start chatting.",
105:           "",
106:           `Open this link and sign in: ${device.verification_uri_complete}`,
107:           "",
108:           `Or go to ${device.verification_uri} and enter the code ${device.user_code}`,
109:           "",
110:           `The code expires in ${Math.round(device.expires_in / 60)} minutes.`,
111:         ].join("\n")
112:       )
113:     );
114: 
115:     // Counted in whole intervals rather than clock time, so replays of this function agree on how
116:     // far along the flow is
117:     let interval = Math.max(device.interval, 5);
118:     for (let waited = 0, poll = 0; waited < device.expires_in; poll++) {
119:       await step.sleep(`wait-${poll}`, `${interval}s`);
120:       waited += interval;
121: 
122:       const result: PollResult = await step.run(`poll-${poll}`, async (): Promise<PollResult> => {
123:         const token = await pollToken(device.device_code);
124:         if ("access_token" in token) {
125:           const user = await fetchUserInfo(token.access_token);
126:           // The unique index keeps an existing link if two flows for the same user both finish
127:           await db
128:             .insert(channelIdentifiers)
129:             .values({ platform, externalUserId, clerkUserId: user.sub })
130:             .onConflictDoNothing();
131:           const link = await findChannelLink(platform, externalUserId);
132:           // The platform handle isn't logged (a Linq handle is a phone number or email): the
133:           // Clerk id identifies the user
134:           if (link) Sentry.setUser({ id: link.clerkUserId });
135:           log.info("Account linked", {
136:             "channel.platform": platform,
137:             // False when a concurrent flow linked this handle to another Clerk user first
138:             "account.link_new": link?.clerkUserId === user.sub,
139:           });
140:           return { status: "linked", clerkUserId: link?.clerkUserId, email: user.email };
141:         }
142:         switch (token.error) {
143:           case "authorization_pending":
144:             return { status: "pending" };
145:           case "slow_down":
146:             return { status: "slow_down" };
147:           case "access_denied":
148:             return { status: "denied" };
149:           case "expired_token":
150:             return { status: "expired" };
151:           default:
152:             throw new NonRetriableError(
153:               `Device flow failed: ${token.error} ${token.error_description ?? ""}`
154:             );
155:         }
156:       });
157: 
158:       switch (result.status) {
159:         case "linked":
160:           await step.run("send-linked", () =>
161:             sendMessage(
162:               `You're connected${result.email ? ` as ${result.email}` : ""}. Send me a message to get started.`
163:             )
164:           );
165:           return { status: "linked" as const, clerkUserId: result.clerkUserId };
166:         case "denied":
167:           log.info("Account linking denied", { "channel.platform": platform });
168:           await step.run("send-denied", () =>
169:             sendMessage("Sign-in was cancelled. Send me any message to try again.")
170:           );
171:           return { status: "denied" as const };
172:         case "expired":
173:           waited = device.expires_in;
174:           break;
175:         case "slow_down":
176:           interval += 5;
177:           break;
178:         case "pending":
179:           break;
180:       }
181:     }
182: 
183:     // Many expiries mean users get the link but don't finish signing in
184:     log.info("Account linking expired", { "channel.platform": platform });
185:     await step.run("send-expired", () =>
186:       sendMessage("The sign-in code expired. Send me any message to get a new one.")
187:     );
188:     return { status: "expired" as const };
189:   }

34: const formatterFor = (timeZone: string) => {
35:   let formatter = formatters.get(timeZone);
36:   if (!formatter) {
37:     formatter = new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-US", {
38:       timeZone,
39:       year: "numeric",
40:       month: "2-digit",
41:       day: "2-digit",
42:       hour: "2-digit",
43:       minute: "2-digit",

45:       hourCycle: "h23",
46:     });
47:     formatters.set(timeZone, formatter);
48:   }
49:   return formatter;

53:   try {
54:     formatterFor(timeZone);
55:     return true;
56:   } catch {
57:     return false;
58:   }

61: const zonedParts = (date: Date, timeZone: string): ZonedParts => {
62:   const values: Record<string, string> = {};
63:   for (const part of formatterFor(timeZone).formatToParts(date)) {
64:     if (part.type !== "literal") values[part.type] = part.value;
65:   }
66:   return {
67:     year: Number(values.year),
68:     month: Number(values.month),
69:     day: Number(values.day),
70:     hour: Number(values.hour),
71:     minute: Number(values.minute),
72:     second: Number(values.second),
73:   };
74: };
75: 
76: const utcParts = (date: Date): ZonedParts => ({
77:   year: date.getUTCFullYear(),
78:   month: date.getUTCMonth() + 1,
79:   day: date.getUTCDate(),
80:   hour: date.getUTCHours(),
81:   minute: date.getUTCMinutes(),
82:   second: date.getUTCSeconds(),
83: });
84: 
85: // How far the zone is from UTC at that instant. Two iterations are enough outside a DST switch;
86: // the extra one settles a wall time close to the switch
87: const offsetAt = (date: Date, timeZone: string): number => {
88:   const parts = zonedParts(date, timeZone);
89:   const asUtc = Date.UTC(
90:     parts.year,
91:     parts.month - 1,
92:     parts.day,
93:     parts.hour,
94:     parts.minute,
95:     parts.second
96:   );
97:   return asUtc - (date.getTime() - date.getMilliseconds());
98: };
99: 
100: export const wallTimeToDate = (parts: ZonedParts, timeZone: string): Date => {
101:   const target = Date.UTC(
102:     parts.year,
103:     parts.month - 1,
104:     parts.day,
105:     parts.hour,
106:     parts.minute,
107:     parts.second
108:   );
109:   let timestamp = target;
110:   for (let i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
111:     const adjusted = target - offsetAt(new Date(timestamp), timeZone);
112:     if (adjusted === timestamp) break;
113:     timestamp = adjusted;
114:   }
115:   return new Date(timestamp);
116: };
117: 
118: export const formatInZone = (
119:   date: Date,
120:   timeZone: string,
121:   options: Intl.DateTimeFormatOptions = { dateStyle: "medium", timeStyle: "short" }
122: ): string => new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-GB", { timeZone, ...options }).format(date);
123: 
124: const FREQUENCIES: Record<RepeatFrequency, number> = {
125:   daily: RRule.DAILY,
126:   weekly: RRule.WEEKLY,
127:   monthly: RRule.MONTHLY,
128:   yearly: RRule.YEARLY,
129: };
130: 
131: // The model uses JavaScript's weekday numbering (0 = Sunday); rrule has its own Weekday objects
132: const WEEKDAYS = [RRule.SU, RRule.MO, RRule.TU, RRule.WE, RRule.TH, RRule.FR, RRule.SA];
133: 
134: // The rule is kept in a floating timeline: its dates carry the user's wall-clock time, not a UTC
135: // instant. nextOccurrence converts the wall time back to a real instant in the stored timezone, so
136: // a 09:00 reminder stays at 09:00 across DST changes
137: export const buildReminderSchedule = (
138:   dueAt: Date,
139:   timeZone: string,
140:   repeat?: ReminderRepeat
141: ): { dueAt: Date; rrule: string | null } => {
142:   if (!repeat) return { dueAt, rrule: null };
143: 
144:   if (repeat.weekdays && repeat.frequency !== "weekly") {
145:     throw new ToolInputError("weekdays only applies to weekly repeats.");
146:   }
147:   if (repeat.dayOfMonth && repeat.frequency !== "monthly") {
148:     throw new ToolInputError("dayOfMonth only applies to monthly repeats.");
149:   }
150:   if (repeat.interval !== undefined && repeat.interval < 1) {
151:     throw new ToolInputError("The repeat interval must be at least 1.");
152:   }
153:   if (repeat.weekdays?.some((day) => day < 0 || day > 6)) {
154:     throw new ToolInputError("weekdays must be between 0 (Sunday) and 6 (Saturday).");
155:   }
156:   if (repeat.dayOfMonth !== undefined && (repeat.dayOfMonth < 1 || repeat.dayOfMonth > 31)) {
157:     throw new ToolInputError("dayOfMonth must be between 1 and 31.");
158:   }
159: 
160:   const local = zonedParts(dueAt, timeZone);
161:   const floatingStart = new Date(
162:     Date.UTC(local.year, local.month - 1, local.day, local.hour, local.minute, local.second)
163:   );
164:   const options: Partial<Options> = {
165:     freq: FREQUENCIES[repeat.frequency],
166:     interval: repeat.interval ?? 1,
167:     dtstart: floatingStart,
168:   };
169:   if (repeat.frequency === "weekly" && repeat.weekdays?.length) {
170:     options.byweekday = [...new Set(repeat.weekdays)].map((day) => WEEKDAYS[day]!);
171:   }
172:   if (repeat.frequency === "monthly" && repeat.dayOfMonth) {
173:     options.bymonthday = repeat.dayOfMonth;
174:   }
175: 
176:   const rule = new RRule(options);
177:   // A first date that doesn't match the rule (a Tuesday for a Mon/Wed repeat) still means the
178:   // first matching occurrence, not a reminder on the wrong day
179:   const firstFloating = rule.after(new Date(floatingStart.getTime() - 1), true);
180:   if (!firstFloating) throw new ToolInputError("That repeat never produces an occurrence.");
181: 
182:   return {
183:     dueAt: wallTimeToDate(utcParts(firstFloating), timeZone),
184:     rrule: rule.toString(),
185:   };
186: };
187: 
188: export const nextOccurrence = (
189:   rruleText: string,
190:   timeZone: string,
191:   after: Date
192: ): Date | null => {
193:   const rule = RRule.fromString(rruleText);
194:   const local = zonedParts(after, timeZone);
195:   const floatingNow = new Date(
196:     Date.UTC(local.year, local.month - 1, local.day, local.hour, local.minute, local.second, after.getMilliseconds())
197:   );
198:   const nextFloating = rule.after(floatingNow, false);
199:   if (!nextFloating) return null;
200:   return wallTimeToDate(utcParts(nextFloating), timeZone);
201: };
202: 
203: // "every week on Monday, Wednesday" for list results and confirmations
204: export const repeatToText = (rruleText: string): string => {
205:   try {
206:     return RRule.fromString(rruleText).toText();
207:   } catch {
208:     return "repeats";
209:   }
210: };

20: type DeliveryResult = {
21:   status: "sent" | "failed" | "skipped";
22:   nextDueAt: string | null;
23: };
24: 
25: // Step results are JSON-serialized. Delivery only needs these fields; the dates are not used here
26: type ClaimedReminder = Pick<
27:   Reminder,
28:   "id" | "platform" | "chatId" | "text" | "attempts" | "rrule" | "timezone"
29: >;
30: 
31: const scheduleNext = async (reminder: Pick<Reminder, "id">, nextDueAt: Date) => {
32:   try {
33:     await scheduleReminderEvent({ id: reminder.id, dueAt: nextDueAt });
34:     return true;
35:   } catch (error) {
36:     // The fallback sweep delivers the next occurrence if its event is missing. Scheduling is
37:     // deliberately not a separate step: one reminder occurrence should stay at three executions
38:     log.error("Could not schedule next reminder occurrence", {
39:       "reminder.id": reminder.id,
40:       "error.name": error instanceof Error ? error.name : "unknown",
41:     });
42:     return false;
43:   }
44: };
45: 
46: const deliverClaimedReminder = async (
47:   reminder: ClaimedReminder,
48:   dueAt: Date
49: ): Promise<DeliveryResult> => {
50:   // If the event fires a little early, the next occurrence must still be after this one. If
51:   // delivery is late, skip occurrences that already passed
52:   const after = new Date(Math.max(Date.now(), dueAt.getTime()));
53:   try {
54:     await CHANNELS[reminder.platform].sendText(reminder.chatId, `Reminder: ${reminder.text}`);
55:   } catch (error) {
56:     const attempts = reminder.attempts + 1;
57:     const final = attempts >= MAX_REMINDER_ATTEMPTS;
58:     if (final && reminder.rrule) {
59:       const next = nextOccurrence(reminder.rrule, reminder.timezone, after);
60:       if (next) {
61:         await skipReminderOccurrence(reminder.id, next);
62:         await scheduleNext(reminder, next);
63:         log.error("Reminder occurrence skipped after repeated failures", {
64:           "reminder.id": reminder.id,
65:           "reminder.attempts": attempts,
66:           "channel.platform": reminder.platform,
67:           "error.name": error instanceof Error ? error.name : "unknown",
68:         });
69:         return { status: "failed", nextDueAt: next.toISOString() };
70:       }
71:     }
72: 
73:     await recordReminderFailure(reminder.id, attempts);
74:     const fields = {
75:       "reminder.id": reminder.id,
76:       "reminder.attempts": attempts,
77:       "channel.platform": reminder.platform,
78:       "error.name": error instanceof Error ? error.name : "unknown",
79:     };
80:     if (final) log.error("Reminder delivery abandoned", fields);
81:     else log.warn("Reminder delivery failed", fields);
82:     return { status: "failed", nextDueAt: null };
83:   }
84: 
85:   const next = reminder.rrule
86:     ? nextOccurrence(reminder.rrule, reminder.timezone, after)
87:     : null;
88:   await markReminderSent(reminder.id, next);
89:   if (next) await scheduleNext(reminder, next);
90:   log.info("Reminder delivered", {
91:     "reminder.id": reminder.id,
92:     "reminder.recurring": Boolean(reminder.rrule),
93:     "channel.platform": reminder.platform,
94:   });
95:   return { status: "sent", nextDueAt: next?.toISOString() ?? null };
96: };
97: 
98: // The normal delivery path: Inngest holds one delayed event per occurrence and starts this
99: // function only when it is due. Three executions per occurrence: run, claim, deliver
100: export const deliverReminder = inngest.createFunction(
101:   {
102:     id: "deliver-reminder",
103:     triggers: [reminderDue],
104:     retries: 2,
105:     onFailure: async ({ error, event }) => {
106:       await reportFailure(error, {
107:         function: "deliver-reminder",
108:         runId: event.data.run_id,
109:       });
110:     },
111:   },
112:   async ({ event, step }) => {
113:     const scheduledFor = new Date(event.data.scheduledFor);
114:     if (Number.isNaN(scheduledFor.getTime())) return { status: "skipped" as const };
115: 
116:     const reminder = await step.run("claim", () =>
117:       claimReminder(event.data.reminderId, scheduledFor)
118:     );
119:     if (!reminder) return { status: "skipped" as const };
120: 
121:     return step.run("deliver", () => deliverClaimedReminder(reminder, scheduledFor));
122:   }
123: );
124: 
125: // Safety net for a missing delayed event or a recurring occurrence whose next event was not
126: // scheduled. It only wakes once an hour; the normal path above is event-driven
127: export const sweepReminders = inngest.createFunction(
128:   {
129:     id: "sweep-reminders",
130:     triggers: { cron: "0 * * * *" },
131:     retries: 2,
132:     onFailure: async ({ error, event }) => {
133:       await reportFailure(error, { function: "sweep-reminders", runId: event.data.run_id });
134:     },
135:   },
136:   async ({ step }) => {
137:     const due = await step.run("claim-reminders", () => claimDueReminders(BATCH_SIZE));
138:     const results: DeliveryResult[] = [];
139:     for (const reminder of due) {
140:       const dueAt = new Date(reminder.dueAt);
141:       results.push(
142:         await step.run(`deliver-${reminder.id}`, () => deliverClaimedReminder(reminder, dueAt))
143:       );
144:     }
145:     return {
146:       delivered: results.filter((result) => result.status === "sent").length,
147:       failed: results.filter((result) => result.status === "failed").length,
148:       skipped: results.filter((result) => result.status === "skipped").length,
149:     };
150:   }
151: );

12: export type OutgoingFile = { data: Uint8Array; filename: string; mediaType: string };
13: 
14: // The message Telegram shows while the agent works. It is deleted before the reply goes out
15: export const WORKING_TEXT = "💭 Thinking…";
16: 
17: // What the agent needs from a chat platform. Chat ids travel as strings so every platform fits the
18: // same event and table columns. Adding a platform to PLATFORMS makes CHANNELS fail to compile until
19: // it has an entry
20: export type Channel = {
21:   // How the platform is named to the model, e.g. "Telegram" or "iMessage"
22:   platformName: string;
23:   maxSendBytes: number;
24:   sendText: (chatId: string, text: string) => Promise<void>;
25:   sendFile: (chatId: string, file: OutgoingFile, caption?: string) => Promise<void>;
26:   downloadAttachment: (attachmentId: string) => Promise<Uint8Array>;
27:   // The assistant started or finished working on a turn: read receipt and typing bubble, or a
28:   // message that is deleted again. Must never throw: it is cosmetic and must not fail a turn
29:   setWorking: (chatId: string, working: boolean) => Promise<void>;
30:   // Whether setWorking(true) posts a message the user sees (Telegram) rather than only a native
31:   // typing bubble (iMessage). The webhook shows it as soon as a message is queued
32:   workingMessage: boolean;
33: };
34: 
35: // Telegram rejects messages over 4096 characters
36: const TELEGRAM_MAX_LENGTH = 4096;
37: // iMessage has no practical length cap, but a wall of text is hard to read on a phone, so very long
38: // replies go out as several bubbles
39: const LINQ_MAX_LENGTH = 4000;
40: 
41: export const CHANNELS: Record<Platform, Channel> = {
42:   telegram: {
43:     platformName: "Telegram",
44:     maxSendBytes: telegram.MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES,
45:     sendText: async (chatId, text) => {
46:       for (const chunk of splitMessage(text, TELEGRAM_MAX_LENGTH)) {
47:         await telegram.sendMessage(Number(chatId), chunk);
48:       }
49:     },
50:     sendFile: async (chatId, file, caption) => {
51:       await telegram.sendFile(Number(chatId), file, caption);
52:     },
53:     downloadAttachment: telegram.downloadFile,
54:     workingMessage: true,
55:     setWorking: async (chatId, working) => {
56:       const id = Number(chatId);
57:       try {
58:         if (working) {
59:           // Already showing: every model call renews the state, but only the first one sends
60:           if (await findWorkingMessage("telegram", chatId)) return;
61:           const messageId = await telegram.sendMessage(id, WORKING_TEXT, {
62:             disableNotification: true,
63:           });
64:           try {
65:             const saved = await saveWorkingMessage({
66:               platform: "telegram",
67:               chatId,
68:               messageId: String(messageId),
69:             });
70:             // Another caller recorded one first: the message just sent is a duplicate
71:             if (!saved) await telegram.deleteMessage(id, messageId);
72:           } catch (error) {
73:             // Never leave a message behind that no row can delete
74:             await telegram.deleteMessage(id, messageId).catch(() => {});
75:             throw error;
76:           }
77:         } else {
78:           const messageId = await takeWorkingMessage("telegram", chatId);
79:           if (messageId) await telegram.deleteMessage(id, Number(messageId));
80:         }
81:       } catch (error) {
82:         // Cosmetic: a failed working message must not fail the turn
83:         log.warn("Working message failed", {
84:           "channel.platform": "telegram",
85:           "error.name": error instanceof Error ? error.name : "unknown",
86:         });
87:       }
88:     },
89:   },
90:   linq: {
91:     platformName: "iMessage",
92:     maxSendBytes: linq.MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES,
93:     sendText: async (chatId, text) => {
94:       try {
95:         for (const chunk of splitMessage(text, LINQ_MAX_LENGTH)) {
96:           await linq.sendMessage(chatId, chunk);
97:         }
98:       } catch (error) {
99:         // The user texted STOP mid-turn: Linq blocks every send until they write again. Retrying,
100:         // or sending from another line, would break that, so the reply is dropped
101:         if (!linq.isOptedOutError(error)) throw error;
102:         log.warn("Reply dropped: recipient opted out", { "channel.platform": "linq" });
103:       }
104:     },
105:     sendFile: async (chatId, file, caption) => {
106:       await linq.sendFile(chatId, file, caption);
107:     },
108:     downloadAttachment: linq.downloadAttachment,
109:     // Linq's delete only removes the message from Linq's records, not from the recipient's thread,
110:     // so iMessage keeps the native typing bubble instead of a message that would have to stay
111:     workingMessage: false,
112:     setWorking: async (chatId, working) => {
113:       try {
114:         if (working) await Promise.all([linq.markRead(chatId), linq.startTyping(chatId)]);
115:         else await linq.stopTyping(chatId);
116:       } catch (error) {
117:         // Cosmetic: a failed typing bubble must not fail the turn
118:         log.warn("Typing indicator failed", {
119:           "channel.platform": "linq",
120:           "error.name": error instanceof Error ? error.name : "unknown",
121:         });
122:       }
123:     },
124:   },
125: };
126: 
127: // Plain-text sends for flows outside a turn, such as account linking
128: export const sendChannelText = (platform: Platform, chatId: string, text: string) =>
129:   CHANNELS[platform].sendText(chatId, text);

Read file C:\Users\bijim\eek1\README.md, lines 1-141
1: # Personal assistant
2: 
3: A chat assistant on Telegram. iMessage (through Linq) is built in but switched off; see
4: [docs/linq.md](docs/linq.md). Users link their chat account to a Clerk
5: account, and Pro subscribers talk to an agent that can browse the web and use its own Linux computer
6: (Daytona).
7: 
8: ## How it works
9: 
10: ```
11: Telegram (and Linq, off) ──webhook──▶ Hono (Vercel) ──▶ Postgres inbox ──event──▶ Inngest ──▶ /api/inngest
12:                               verify + gate                                           chat-turn, link, sweeper, reminders
13: ```
14: 
15: - **Hono** (`src/app.ts`) takes the platforms' webhooks, verifies them and runs the channel gate
16:   (`src/channel-gate.ts`). A message from a linked Pro user is written to `chat_inbox` and announced
17:   to Inngest. Unlinked users get a sign-in link, and linked users without Pro get an upgrade link.
18: - **Inngest** runs the work as functions in `src/inngest/`, served at `/api/inngest`:
19:   - `chat-turn`: one agent turn at a time per user. Every model call and tool call is a durable
20:     step. Long shell commands run in the background of the Daytona sandbox and are checked every
21:     30 seconds while the run sleeps, so no single request waits on them.
22:   - `link-channel-account`: Clerk device-flow sign-in for new users.
23:   - `sweep-inbox`: every 15 minutes, restarts conversations whose messages nobody is working on.
24:   - `deliver-reminder`: sends one reminder when its delayed `reminder/due` event fires, then
25:     schedules the next occurrence of a recurring reminder.
26:   - `sweep-reminders`: hourly fallback that delivers overdue reminders if a delayed event was
27:     lost or a recurring reminder's next event was not scheduled.
28: - **Postgres** (Neon, Drizzle) holds channel links, the inbox, per-user locks, conversation
29:   transcripts, profiles and reminders. Change the schema in `src/db/schema.ts` and apply it with
30:   `npm run db:push`.
31: - **Vercel** hosts it: `src/index.ts` default-exports the Hono app. `src/local.ts` is the plain
32:   Node server used by `npm run dev` and `npm start`.
33: 
34: ## Run it locally
35: 
36: Requirements: Node 20 or newer, a Postgres database (a Neon branch works well), and the API keys
37: for the services you want to exercise.
38: 
39: 1. Install and create your env file:
40: 
41:    ```
42:    npm install
43:    cp .env.example .env      # on Windows PowerShell: Copy-Item .env.example .env
44:    ```
45: 
46: 2. Fill in `.env`. See `.env.example` for every variable. For local development:
47:    - Keep `INNGEST_DEV=1` and leave `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY` and `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY` out. The local
48:      Inngest dev server needs no keys.
49:    - Set `DATABASE_URL` (pooled) and `DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED` (direct). Both can be the same URL for a
50:      plain Postgres.
51: 
52: 3. Create the tables:
53: 
54:    ```
55:    npm run db:push
56:    ```
57: 
58: 4. Start the app and the Inngest dev server in two terminals:
59:

62:    npm run inngest:dev    # the Inngest dev server and its UI on http://localhost:8288
63:    ```

66:    http://localhost:8288 to see the registered functions, events and step-by-step runs.

69:    - `curl localhost:3000/api/inngest` should report `"mode":"dev"` and `"function_count":10`
70:      (five functions plus their failure handlers).
71:    - In the Inngest UI, open Functions and invoke or inspect runs.
72:    - `npm test` runs the unit tests. The database tests need `DATABASE_URL` pointing at a database
73:      with the schema pushed, and are skipped without it.
74: 
75: ### Talking to a real chat from your machine
76: 
77: The platforms can only call a public URL, so expose port 3000 with a tunnel (for example
78: `ngrok http 3000`) and register the tunnel URL:
79: 
80: - **Telegram:** call `setWebhook` with the same secret as `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET`:
81: 
82:   ```
83:   curl "https://api.telegram.org/bot$TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN/setWebhook" \
84:     -d "url=https://<tunnel>/telegram/webhook" \
85:     -d "secret_token=$TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET"
86:   ```
87: 
88: - **Linq:** switched off. To try it locally, follow [docs/linq.md](docs/linq.md).
89: 
90: Use a separate bot for development, so you don't take over production traffic. A Telegram bot has
91: only one webhook at a time.
92: 
93: ## Set it up in the cloud
94: 
95: 1. **Database.** Create a Neon project and put its pooled connection string in `DATABASE_URL` and
96:    its direct one in `DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED`. Run `npm run db:push` once against it from your
97:    machine before the first deploy.
98: 
99: 2. **Third-party services.** Create the accounts and keys listed in `.env.example`:
100:    - Clerk: a confidential OAuth app with the Device Authorization Grant enabled (for account
101:      linking), plus billing with a `pro` plan. Users without an active Pro subscription are asked
102:      to upgrade.
103:    - Telegram bot, Daytona, Firecrawl, and an OpenAI-compatible API for the model
104:      (`OPENAI_BASEURL`, `OPENAI_MODEL`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`). Linq is off, so it needs no setup (see
105:      [docs/linq.md](docs/linq.md)).
106:    - Sentry (optional): set `SENTRY_DSN`.
107: 
108: 3. **Vercel.**
109:    - Import the repository. Keep Fluid compute on (the default), which allows 300 seconds per
110:      request on Hobby. `checkpointing.maxRuntime` in `src/inngest/client.ts` stays below that.
111:    - Add every variable from `.env.example` in the project's environment settings, except
112:      `INNGEST_DEV`, which stays unset in production.
113:    - Turn off Deployment Protection for the project, or give Inngest the bypass secret in the
114:      integration settings (next step). Protection blocks Inngest's calls to `/api/inngest`.
115: 
116: 4. **Inngest Cloud.**
117:    - Create an account and install Inngest's Vercel integration for the project. It sets
118:      `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY` and `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY` on Vercel for you and syncs the functions on each
119:      deploy. Without the integration, create an event key and copy the signing key from the Inngest
120:      dashboard, add both in Vercel, and sync the app from Inngest's Apps page using
121:      `https://<your-domain>/api/inngest`.
122:    - After a deploy, the app should appear under Apps with all ten functions, including the
123:      `sweep-inbox` and `sweep-reminders` crons.
124: 
125: 5. **Webhooks.** Point the platforms at your production domain, using the same secrets as your
126:    Vercel variables:
127:    - Telegram: `setWebhook` with `url=https://<your-domain>/telegram/webhook` and
128:      `secret_token=<TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET>`.
129:    - Linq is off: `/linq/webhook` answers 404 until `LINQ_ENABLED=true` (see docs/linq.md).
130: 
131: 6. **Smoke test.** Message the bot from an unlinked account. You should get a sign-in link. After
132:    signing in, and with a Pro subscription, send another message and watch the run in the Inngest
133:    dashboard.
134: 
135: ### Notes

137: - Keep local and production Inngest keys separate. Never copy production keys into your local

139: - Vercel Hobby is meant for non-commercial use. Check its terms if the app takes payments.
140: - Message text and replies live in Inngest's step state while a run is in flight.
141: - `AGENTS.md` has the conventions for working in this repository.

120: const requireTimezone = async (clerkUserId: string) => {
121:   const profile = await loadProfile(clerkUserId);
122:   if (!profile?.timezone || !isValidTimeZone(profile.timezone)) {

133: export const executeReminderTool = async (
134:   name: ReminderToolName,
135:   rawInput: unknown,
136:   context: ReminderContext
137: ): Promise<ToolOutput> => {
138:   switch (name) {
139:     case "setTimezone": {
140:       const parsed = schemas.setTimezone.safeParse(rawInput);
141:       if (!parsed.success) {
142:         throw new ToolInputError(parsed.error.issues[0]?.message ?? "Invalid timezone.");
143:       }
144:       const { timezone } = parsed.data;
145:       if (!isValidTimeZone(timezone)) {
146:         throw new ToolInputError(
147:           "That is not a valid IANA timezone. Use a name like Europe/London or America/New_York."
148:         );
149:       }
150:       await saveTimezone(context.clerkUserId, timezone);
151:       return json({
152:         timezone,
153:         localTime: formatInZone(new Date(), timezone, {
154:           dateStyle: "full",
155:           timeStyle: "long",
156:         }),
157:       });
158:     }
159:     case "setReminder": {
160:       const parsed = schemas.setReminder.safeParse(rawInput);
161:       if (!parsed.success) {
162:         throw new ToolInputError(parsed.error.issues[0]?.message ?? "Invalid reminder.");
163:       }
164:       const input = parsed.data;
165:       const text = input.text.trim();
166:       if (!text) throw new ToolInputError("The reminder text is empty.");
167:       const timezone = await requireTimezone(context.clerkUserId);
168:       const dueAt = new Date(input.dueAt);
169:       if (dueAt.getTime() < Date.now() - 60_000) {
170:         throw new ToolInputError("The reminder time is in the past. Choose a future date and time.");
171:       }
172: 
173:       let schedule: ReturnType<typeof buildReminderSchedule>;
174:       try {
175:         schedule = buildReminderSchedule(dueAt, timezone, input.repeat);
176:       } catch (error) {
177:         if (error instanceof ToolInputError) throw error;
178:         throw new ToolInputError("That repeat is not valid.");
179:       }
180:       if (schedule.dueAt.getTime() < Date.now() - 60_000) {
181:         throw new ToolInputError("The reminder time is in the past. Choose a future date and time.");
182:       }
183: 
184:       const dedupeKey = `${context.platform}:${context.chatId}:${context.toolCallId}`;
185:       let reminder = await loadReminderByDedupeKey(dedupeKey);
186:       if (!reminder) {
187:         const pending = await countPendingReminders(context.clerkUserId);
188:         if (pending >= MAX_PENDING_REMINDERS) {
189:           throw new ToolInputError(
190:             `The user already has ${MAX_PENDING_REMINDERS} active reminders. Cancel one first.`
191:           );
192:         }
193:         reminder = await insertReminder({
194:           clerkUserId: context.clerkUserId,
195:           platform: context.platform,
196:           chatId: context.chatId,
197:           dedupeKey,
198:           text,
199:           dueAt: schedule.dueAt,
200:           rrule: schedule.rrule,
201:           timezone,
202:         });
203:       }
204: 
205:       // If this fails, the reminder is still stored and the hourly sweep will deliver it. The
206:       // idempotent event id means a retried tool step cannot schedule it twice
207:       try {
208:         await scheduleReminderEvent(reminder);
209:       } catch (error) {
210:         log.error("Reminder created but its due event was not scheduled", {
211:           "reminder.id": reminder.id,
212:           "error.name": error instanceof Error ? error.name : "unknown",
213:         });
214:       }
215: 
216:       return json({
217:         id: reminder.id,
218:         text: reminder.text,
219:         dueAt: reminder.dueAt.toISOString(),
220:         localTime: localTime(reminder.dueAt, reminder.timezone),
221:         timezone: reminder.timezone,
222:         repeats: reminder.rrule ? repeatToText(reminder.rrule) : null,
223:       });
224:     }
225:     case "listReminders": {
226:       const parsed = schemas.listReminders.safeParse(rawInput);
227:       if (!parsed.success) {
228:         throw new ToolInputError("Invalid request.");
229:       }
230:       const rows = await listPendingReminders(context.clerkUserId);
231:       return json({
232:         reminders: rows.map((reminder) => ({
233:           id: reminder.id,
234:           text: reminder.text,
235:           dueAt: reminder.dueAt.toISOString(),
236:           localTime: localTime(reminder.dueAt, reminder.timezone),
237:           timezone: reminder.timezone,
238:           repeats: reminder.rrule ? repeatToText(reminder.rrule) : null,
239:         })),
240:       });
241:     }
242:     case "cancelReminder": {
243:       const parsed = schemas.cancelReminder.safeParse(rawInput);
244:       if (!parsed.success) {
245:         throw new ToolInputError(parsed.error.issues[0]?.message ?? "Invalid reminder id.");
246:       }
247:       const canceled = await cancelReminder(context.clerkUserId, parsed.data.id);
248:       if (!canceled) {
249:         throw new ToolInputError(
250:           "No active reminder has that id. Use listReminders to see the current reminders."
251:         );
252:       }
253:       return json({ canceled: true, id: parsed.data.id });
254:     }
255:   }

258: export const reminderToolsInstructions = `
259: ## Reminders
260: 
261: You can create, list and cancel reminders, including recurring ones. The app delivers them to the chat where they were created, even after the conversation ends.
262: 
263: - Before scheduling, make sure you know the user's IANA timezone. If the system prompt says it is unknown, ask for their city or timezone and call setTimezone first.
264: - setReminder takes the first occurrence as an ISO 8601 timestamp with an explicit UTC offset, expressed in the user's local time. For example, 2026-11-10T09:00:00+03:00.
265: - For a recurring reminder, pass repeat. frequency is daily, weekly, monthly or yearly; interval means every N periods; weekdays (0 = Sunday ... 6 = Saturday) is for weekly schedules; dayOfMonth is for monthly schedules. Every weekday is weekly with weekdays [1,2,3,4,5].
266: - Omit repeat for a one-off reminder. If the first date does not match the repeat (for example a Tuesday for a Monday/Wednesday schedule), the first matching occurrence is used.
267: - When confirming, state the local date, time and repeat in plain language. Don't show ids unless the user asks.
268: - To change a reminder, list it, cancel it and create a new one. Never guess an id: list first.

## What I'd build

**Server-rendered dashboard with hono/jsx** (tsconfig already has `jsxImportSource: hono/jsx`), mounted as a Hono sub-app `src/dashboard/` under `/dashboard`. Zero or near-zero new dependencies (auth via the existing Clerk OAuth app, cookie signing via node:crypto, CSS as one shared static route). Same repo, same deploy, same DB.

### Pages
1. Overview (`/dashboard`) — linked channels, Pro status, timezone, next 5 reminders in user's tz, recent activity (last processed inbox messages + reminder deliveries), quota card (active reminders x/50), upgrade card if not Pro.
2. Reminders (`/dashboard/reminders`) — tabs Upcoming / Paused / Past. New + Edit forms. Actions: pause/resume/cancel (POST buttons). Next occurrence formatted in user tz; repeat description via `repeatToText`.
3. Settings (`/dashboard/settings`) — timezone (validated with isValidTimeZone), linked channels list, sign out, delete data (confirm page).

### New backend bits (reused where available)
- `reminders` status gains "paused" (TS-level; text column, no DB constraint change) + db helpers pauseReminder/resumeReminder/loadUserReminder/updateReminder/listSent-ish.
- Paused reminders excluded from sweeps (they already only claim pending). Pending delayed event fires → claim rejects → skipped. Resume recomputes next occurrence and reschedules a fresh event (new event id salt so Inngest dedupe doesn't swallow it).
- Editing a reminder: update text/dueAt/rrule/timezone → rebuild via existing buildReminderSchedule → reschedule event.
- channelIdentifiers gains chatId + lastSeenAt (so dashboard-created reminders know where to deliver; gate persists chatId on allowed messages; backfill from chatInbox).
- Pro status via existing hasProPlan; Purchase = redirect to the Clerk Account Portal billing (existing upgradeUrl()); "Manage" for Pro.

### Auth
Session cookie, HttpOnly + HMAC-signed,UserId-only contents. Sign-in via Clerk.

Also new env: APP_URL (for links + OAuth redirect URI).

### Plan for tests
Unit tests for session seal/verify, schedule helpers, form parsing. Follow existing *.test.ts style.

### Out of scope / later
- In-dashboard Stripe/checkout (Clerk Billing components) — v1 links out to the Account Portal.
- Pause/edit via chat agent tools (small follow-up using same db helpers).
- Message-usage counters (quota beyond reminders).

1. Sign-in Approach: (a) Auth-code grant on existing Clerk OAuth app (recommended; 1 CLI command) (b) reuse device flow (no Clerk change, clunkier).

- **No pause/edit exists yet** — the chat agent can create/list/cancel; editing isn't even possible in chat (only cancel + recreate). Pause is brand new and needs a small schema + sweeper-aware change. So your "are editing and pause possible?" answer: they're *possible*, but entirely new work beyond UI.
- **Quota** today = max 50 pending reminders (enforced in the agent tool). There's no message-usage counter in the schema.

## Dashboard (server-rendered with hono/jsx, same app, same deploy)

New `src/dashboard/` sub-app mounted at `/dashboard`. Almost no new dependencies — auth via the existing Clerk OAuth app, session cookie signed with `node:crypto`, one shared CSS route, reuses `rrule`, `zod`, existing db helpers.

**1. Overview** — Pro status + plan card (Free vs Pro, quota: active reminders X/50), linked Telegram/Linq channels, timezone, next 5 reminders formatted in the user's tz, recent activity (last processed inbox messages + recently sent/failed reminders).

**2. Reminders** — tabs Upcoming / Paused / Past; create form (text, datetime, channel, repeat: none/daily/weekly/monthly/yearly + interval/weekdays/day-of-month — same model the agent uses via `buildReminderSchedule`); edit text/time/repeat; pause/resume/cancel buttons (POST forms). Next occurrence always shown in the user's timezone.

**3. Settings** — timezone picker (validated with existing `isValidTimeZone`), linked channels with unlink, sign out, delete data with a confirmation page (wipes reminders, transcripts, inbox, profile, channel links → user re-links via chat).

**Under the hood** (the parts that aren't "just UI"):
- `paused` status on reminders (text column, no DB constraint change) + `pause`/`resume`/`update` db functions. Sweeps already only touch `pending`, so paused reminders are naturally skipped; a stale delayed event becomes a harmless no-op. Resume recomputes the next occurrence and schedules a fresh event (salted event id so Inngest's dedupe doesn't swallow it).
- Store `chatId` + `lastSeenAt` on `channel_identifiers` (persisted at link time and on each gated message, backfilled from the inbox) so reminders created in the dashboard know which chat to deliver to.
- New env var `APP_URL` for links/OAuth redirect; a line in the bot's `/start`/`/whoami` pointing users to the dashboard.

## What "agent parity" means here

**Today, the chat agent can only:** create reminders (`setReminder`), list them, cancel them, set timezone. There is no edit and no pause in chat — if a user says "move my gym reminder to 7pm," the model has to cancel the existing one and create a new one (which is what the prompt currently instructs).

**The dashboard work adds new database helpers:** `pauseReminder`, `resumeReminder`, `updateReminder` (with proper re-scheduling of the Inngest event, the paused status, resume-recalculation logic, etc.).

The question is: once those helpers exist, do we *also* wrap them as new agent tools in `src/inngest/reminder-tools.ts` (e.g. `pauseReminder`, `resumeReminder`, and an `editReminder` tool), so the same abilities work in chat?

**Pros of including it:**
- Users can naturally say "pause my 9am one" or "make that weekly instead of daily" in chat — the primary interface of this product. A true edit keeps the reminder's id, series and delivery history intact, instead of cancel + recreate.
- The wrappers are thin (~80 lines including prompt-text updates) on top of helpers that will already be written and tested.

**Cons / why to split it out:**
- Bigger blast radius in one pass: agent prompt text changes → model behavior changes, needs its own tests, slight risk of model misuse (e.g. pausing instead of canceling).
- Your stated goal is the dashboard; shipping it first gets the feature in front of users sooner, agent tools can land as a quick follow-up with zero dashboard changes.

---
title: Get Started
description: A quick guide to adding your first component.
---

This guide provides the essentials for adding **coss ui** components to your React application.

## Prerequisites

Our components are built with [Tailwind CSS v4](https://tailwindcss.com). Before you begin, make sure you have a React project set up with Tailwind CSS.

## Adding Components

You can add components **automatically with the shadcn CLI** or **manually by copying the files**. Both methods work for primitives, particles, and atoms.

### With the CLI

Each component page provides a command to add it to your project automatically. The CLI will create the necessary files and install any dependencies for you.

### New Project Setup (Recommended)

For new projects, use the **coss style** preset to initialize everything in one command:

```bash
npx shadcn@latest init @coss/style
```

This installs all UI components, the neutral color system, sidebar variables, and base styles. It also installs **Inter** (`--font-sans`, `--font-heading`) and **Geist Mono** (`--font-mono`) as default fonts, automatically configured in your `layout.tsx`.

### Existing Projects

To add components to an existing project:

```bash
npx shadcn@latest add @coss/ui
```

This adds all UI primitives (button, card, avatar, dialog, tabs, and more). For the full theme including colors, sidebar, and fonts:

```bash
npx shadcn@latest add @coss/style
```

Or add only the color tokens:

```bash
npx shadcn@latest add @coss/ui @coss/colors-neutral
```

See the [Styling](/ui/docs/styling) guide for more details.

### Manually

1.  **Find a component** on the [Components](/ui/docs) or [Particles](/ui/particles) pages.
2.  **Copy the code** from the **Code** tab.
3.  **Create a new file** in your project (e.g., `components/ui/button.tsx`) and paste the code.
4.  **Install any dependencies** listed on the component's page.
5.  **Import and use** the component in your app.

## Styling

Components are styled with the design token system we use at **Cal.com**, which is defined by CSS variables and implemented with Tailwind CSS.

The variables are the same as shadcn/ui, and are fully customizable. You can modify them in your global stylesheet (e.g., `app/globals.css`) to match your design system.

We've introduced a few additional tokens to provide more granular control:

- `--destructive-foreground`: Used for destructive-outline buttons, destructive menu items, badges, and field errors
- `--info` and `--info-foreground`: Commonly used for info-colored badges, toast types, and alerts
- `--success` and `--success-foreground`: Used for success-colored badges, toast types, and alerts
- `--warning` and `--warning-foreground`: Used for warning-colored badges, toast types, and alerts

**Important**: If you manually import components, you must also import these additional tokens in your CSS file (e.g., `app/globals.css`). However, if you use the CLI to import components, these tokens will be automatically imported and configured for you.

## Fonts

coss components use three CSS custom properties for typography:

- `--font-sans` — Body text, buttons, labels, and most UI elements
- `--font-mono` — Code blocks, `<kbd>`, monospace text
- `--font-heading` — Dialog and AlertDialog titles (`--font-heading: var(--font-sans)` in theme)

When you use `@coss/style`, `@coss/fonts` is installed automatically — Inter for `--font-sans` and `--font-heading`, Geist Mono for `--font-mono` — all wired in your `layout.tsx`.

coss.com uses **Cal Sans 2.0** variable from `@coss/ui/fonts` for both body and headings. By default the theme aliases `--font-heading` to `--font-sans`, so one `--font-sans` variable is enough. You can wire a separate `--font-heading` when titles should use a different family — see [Styling — Font Variables](/ui/docs/styling#font-variables).

### Custom fonts

To use different fonts, ensure the `variable` option in your `next/font` configuration matches the expected CSS variables:

```tsx
import { Inter } from "next/font/google";
import { JetBrains_Mono } from "next/font/google";

const inter = Inter({ variable: "--font-sans", subsets: ["latin"] });
const jetbrainsMono = JetBrains_Mono({ variable: "--font-mono", subsets: ["latin"] });
```

When sans and heading share one family, only `--font-sans` needs to be set — the theme aliases `--font-heading` to it.

For a separate heading font, add `--font-heading` in your layout and update the theme token:

```tsx
const interDisplay = Inter({ variable: "--font-heading", subsets: ["latin"], weight: "700" });

<body className={`${inter.variable} ${interDisplay.variable} ${jetbrainsMono.variable} font-sans`}>
```

```css
@theme inline {
  --font-heading: var(--font-heading);
}
```

**Note:** Next.js starters default to variable names like `--font-geist-sans`, which don't match coss's expected `--font-sans`. If fonts appear broken after setup, check that the variable names match.

## Primitive exports

Components that wrap Base UI primitives re-export the underlying primitive. Use the styled component when our defaults work, or the primitive when you need different compositions or styling. In a monorepo with a shared UI package, apps import from that package—Base UI stays in one place, no need to add it to each app.

```tsx
import { Slider, SliderValue, SliderPrimitive } from "@coss/ui/components/slider";
```

## Base UI Re-exports

When you only need `useRender`, `mergeProps`, `CSPProvider`, or `DirectionProvider`, import them from `@coss/ui/base-ui/*` instead of adding `@base-ui/react` as a direct dependency:

- `@coss/ui/base-ui/use-render`
- `@coss/ui/base-ui/merge-props`
- `@coss/ui/base-ui/csp-provider`
- `@coss/ui/base-ui/direction-provider`

This is especially useful in **monorepos**: apps and packages that already depend on `@coss/ui` can use these utilities without adding `@base-ui/react` as another dependency. You avoid duplicate installs, keep versions in sync, and simplify your dependency tree—`@base-ui/react` lives in one place (`@coss/ui`), and everything else imports through it.

## Migration from Radix UI

For developers migrating from **Radix UI**, many of our **UI primitives** include instructions and examples for translating equivalent Radix components to Base UI. This ensures a smooth transition while preserving accessibility, behaviors, and API patterns.

## Working with LLMs

We structure the documentation to make the components **AI-friendly**, so language models can understand, reason about, and modify them. To support this, we include:

- **[Agent Skills](/ui/docs/skills)** — Install coss knowledge directly into your AI assistant with `npx skills add cosscom/ui`. Covers all 53 primitives, styling conventions, migration patterns, and particle examples.
- A [llms.txt](/ui/llms.txt) file that provides a map of the documentation and component structure for your AI agent.
- A **Copy Markdown** button on every page, so you can easily share content or feed it to your AI workflows. 
---
title: Styling
description: A guide to styling components with our color system.
---

## Overview

This UI library is optimized to work with specific color tokens that provide crisp, contrasted borders and enhanced visual depth. Our styling system builds upon [shadcn/ui's CSS variables approach](https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/theming) to ensure consistency and maintainability.

Our components use opaque borders instead of solid ones to ensure crisp, contrasted borders even when backgrounds lack sufficient contrast. These opaque borders mix with bottom shadows to create enhanced contrast and visual depth.

While this color system is optional, it's designed to provide optimal visual results. Using conventional color tokens may result in inconsistent borders and shadows that don't achieve the intended design quality.

## Installation

See [Get Started](/ui/docs/get-started) for installing with the CLI. If you prefer to add the theme manually, paste the following into your `globals.css`:

```css title="app/globals.css"
@theme inline {
  --font-sans: var(--font-sans);
  --font-heading: var(--font-sans);
  --font-mono: var(--font-mono);
  --color-background: var(--background);
  --color-foreground: var(--foreground);
  --color-sidebar-ring: var(--sidebar-ring);
  --color-sidebar-border: var(--sidebar-border);
  --color-sidebar-accent-foreground: var(--sidebar-accent-foreground);
  --color-sidebar-accent: var(--sidebar-accent);
  --color-sidebar-primary-foreground: var(--sidebar-primary-foreground);
  --color-sidebar-primary: var(--sidebar-primary);
  --color-sidebar-foreground: var(--sidebar-foreground);
  --color-sidebar: var(--sidebar);
  --color-ring: var(--ring);
  --color-input: var(--input);
  --color-border: var(--border);
  --color-destructive: var(--destructive);
  --color-destructive-foreground: var(--destructive-foreground);
  --color-info: var(--info);
  --color-info-foreground: var(--info-foreground);
  --color-success: var(--success);
  --color-success-foreground: var(--success-foreground);
  --color-warning: var(--warning);
  --color-warning-foreground: var(--warning-foreground);
  --color-accent-foreground: var(--accent-foreground);
  --color-accent: var(--accent);
  --color-muted-foreground: var(--muted-foreground);
  --color-muted: var(--muted);
  --color-secondary-foreground: var(--secondary-foreground);
  --color-secondary: var(--secondary);
  --color-primary-foreground: var(--primary-foreground);
  --color-primary: var(--primary);
  --color-popover-foreground: var(--popover-foreground);
  --color-popover: var(--popover);
  --color-card-foreground: var(--card-foreground);
  --color-card: var(--card);
  --animate-skeleton: skeleton 2s -1s infinite linear;
  @keyframes skeleton {
    to {
      background-position: -200% 0;
    }
  }
}

:root {
  --radius: 0.625rem;
  --background: var(--color-white);
  --foreground: var(--color-neutral-800);
  --card: var(--color-white);
  --card-foreground: var(--color-neutral-800);
  --popover: var(--color-white);

  --secondary-foreground: var(--color-neutral-800);

  --muted-foreground: color-mix(
    in srgb,
    var(--color-neutral-500) 90%,
    var(--color-black)

  --accent: --alpha(var(--color-black) / 4%);

  --success-foreground: var(--color-emerald-700);
  --warning: var(--color-amber-500);
  --warning-foreground: var(--color-amber-700);
  --border: --alpha(var(--color-black) / 8%);
  --input: --alpha(var(--color-black) / 10%);
  --ring: var(--color-neutral-400);

  --sidebar-foreground: color-mix(
    in srgb,
    var(--color-neutral-800) 64%,
    var(--sidebar)

  --sidebar-primary: var(--color-neutral-800);

## Font Variables

Components like Dialog, AlertDialog, Sheet, and Empty use the `font-heading` class for titles. The coss style preset defines `--font-sans`, `--font-heading`, and `--font-mono` with system font fallbacks.

When you install `@coss/style` via the CLI, Inter is used for `--font-sans` and `--font-heading`, with Geist Mono for `--font-mono`. coss.com uses **Cal Sans 2.0** variable from `@coss/ui/fonts` for both body and headings.

By default, `--font-heading` is aliased to `--font-sans` in the theme. Pair `font-heading` with a weight utility such as `font-bold` or `font-semibold` where needed.

### Custom fonts

To use your own fonts, define the variables in your layout with `next/font`:

```tsx title="app/layout.tsx"
import { fontMono, fontSans } from "@coss/ui/fonts";

export default function RootLayout({ children }) {
  return (
    <html lang="en">
      <body className={`${fontSans.variable} ${fontMono.variable} font-sans`}>
        {children}
      </body>
    </html>
  );
}
```

When sans and heading share one family, only `--font-sans` needs to be set — the theme aliases `--font-heading` to it.

For a separate heading font, wire `--font-heading` in your layout and point the theme token at it:

```tsx title="app/layout.tsx"
import { fontHeading, fontMono, fontSans } from "@coss/ui/fonts";

<body className={`${fontSans.variable} ${fontHeading.variable} ${fontMono.variable} font-sans`}>
```

```css title="app/globals.css"
@theme inline {
  --font-heading: var(--font-heading);
}
```

Apply the variables to `html` or `body` so they cascade. The `font-sans` class sets the default body font.

## Base UI Setup

Since this library is built on Base UI, you need to configure CSS isolation to ensure portaled components (Dialogs, Popovers, Selects, etc.) render correctly above page content.

### Application Root Isolation

Add `isolation: isolate` to your application's root wrapper div. This creates a separate stacking context so portaled components always appear above page contents without z-index conflicts.

```tsx title="app/layout.tsx"
<body>
  <div className="isolate relative flex min-h-svh flex-col">
    {children}
  </div>
</body>
```

### iOS Safari Compatibility

For iOS Safari 26+ compatibility, add `position: relative` to the body element. This ensures backdrops properly cover the visual viewport after page scrolling.

```tsx title="app/layout.tsx"
<body className="relative">
  <div className="isolate relative flex min-h-svh flex-col">
    {children}
  </div>
</body>
```

These styles ensure Base UI components maintain proper rendering hierarchy and visual layering throughout your application. Without isolation, you may experience z-index conflicts where page content appears above modal dialogs or popovers.

---
title: Get Started
description: A quick guide to adding your first component.
---

This guide provides the essentials for adding **coss ui** components to your React application.

## Prerequisites

Our components are built with [Tailwind CSS v4](https://tailwindcss.com). Before you begin, make sure you have a React project set up with Tailwind CSS.

## Adding Components

You can add components **automatically with the shadcn CLI** or **manually by copying the files**. Both methods work for primitives, particles, and atoms.

### With the CLI

Each component page provides a command to add it to your project automatically. The CLI will create the necessary files and install any dependencies for you.

### New Project Setup (Recommended)

For new projects, use the **coss style** preset to initialize everything in one command:

```bash
npx shadcn@latest init @coss/style
```

This installs all UI components, the neutral color system, sidebar variables, and base styles. It also installs **Inter** (`--font-sans`, `--font-heading`) and **Geist Mono** (`--font-mono`) as default fonts, automatically configured in your `layout.tsx`.

### Existing Projects

To add components to an existing project:

```bash
npx shadcn@latest add @coss/ui
```

This adds all UI primitives (button, card, avatar, dialog, tabs, and more). For the full theme including colors, sidebar, and fonts:

```bash
npx shadcn@latest add @coss/style
```

Or add only the color tokens:

```bash
npx shadcn@latest add @coss/ui @coss/colors-neutral
```

See the [Styling](/ui/docs/styling) guide for more details.

### Manually

1.  **Find a component** on the [Components](/ui/docs) or [Particles](/ui/particles) pages.
2.  **Copy the code** from the **Code** tab.
3.  **Create a new file** in your project (e.g., `components/ui/button.tsx`) and paste the code.
4.  **Install any dependencies** listed on the component's page.
5.  **Import and use** the component in your app.

## Styling

Components are styled with the design token system we use at **Cal.com**, which is defined by CSS variables and implemented with Tailwind CSS.

The variables are the same as shadcn/ui, and are fully customizable. You can modify them in your global stylesheet (e.g., `app/globals.css`) to match your design system.

We've introduced a few additional tokens to provide more granular control:

- `--destructive-foreground`: Used for destructive-outline buttons, destructive menu items, badges, and field errors
- `--info` and `--info-foreground`: Commonly used for info-colored badges, toast types, and alerts
- `--success` and `--success-foreground`: Used for success-colored badges, toast types, and alerts
- `--warning` and `--warning-foreground`: Used for warning-colored badges, toast types, and alerts

**Important**: If you manually import components, you must also import these additional tokens in your CSS file (e.g., `app/globals.css`). However, if you use the CLI to import components, these tokens will be automatically imported and configured for you.

## Fonts

coss components use three CSS custom properties for typography:

- `--font-sans` — Body text, buttons, labels, and most UI elements
- `--font-mono` — Code blocks, `<kbd>`, monospace text
- `--font-heading` — Dialog and AlertDialog titles (`--font-heading: var(--font-sans)` in theme)

When you use `@coss/style`, `@coss/fonts` is installed automatically — Inter for `--font-sans` and `--font-heading`, Geist Mono for `--font-mono` — all wired in your `layout.tsx`.

coss.com uses **Cal Sans 2.0** variable from `@coss/ui/fonts` for both body and headings. By default the theme aliases `--font-heading` to `--font-sans`, so one `--font-sans` variable is enough. You can wire a separate `--font-heading` when titles should use a different family — see [Styling — Font Variables](/ui/docs/styling#font-variables).

### Custom fonts

To use different fonts, ensure the `variable` option in your `next/font` configuration matches the expected CSS variables:

```tsx
import { Inter } from "next/font/google";
import { JetBrains_Mono } from "next/font/google";

const inter = Inter({ variable: "--font-sans", subsets: ["latin"] });
const jetbrainsMono = JetBrains_Mono({ variable: "--font-mono", subsets: ["latin"] });
```

When sans and heading share one family, only `--font-sans` needs to be set — the theme aliases `--font-heading` to it.

For a separate heading font, add `--font-heading` in your layout and update the theme token:

```tsx
const interDisplay = Inter({ variable: "--font-heading", subsets: ["latin"], weight: "700" });

<body className={`${inter.variable} ${interDisplay.variable} ${jetbrainsMono.variable} font-sans`}>
```

```css
@theme inline {
  --font-heading: var(--font-heading);
}
```

**Note:** Next.js starters default to variable names like `--font-geist-sans`, which don't match coss's expected `--font-sans`. If fonts appear broken after setup, check that the variable names match.

## Primitive exports

Components that wrap Base UI primitives re-export the underlying primitive. Use the styled component when our defaults work, or the primitive when you need different compositions or styling. In a monorepo with a shared UI package, apps import from that package—Base UI stays in one place, no need to add it to each app.

```tsx
import { Slider, SliderValue, SliderPrimitive } from "@coss/ui/components/slider";
```

## Base UI Re-exports

When you only need `useRender`, `mergeProps`, `CSPProvider`, or `DirectionProvider`, import them from `@coss/ui/base-ui/*` instead of adding `@base-ui/react` as a direct dependency:

- `@coss/ui/base-ui/use-render`
- `@coss/ui/base-ui/merge-props`
- `@coss/ui/base-ui/csp-provider`
- `@coss/ui/base-ui/direction-provider`

This is especially useful in **monorepos**: apps and packages that already depend on `@coss/ui` can use these utilities without adding `@base-ui/react` as another dependency. You avoid duplicate installs, keep versions in sync, and simplify your dependency tree—`@base-ui/react` lives in one place (`@coss/ui`), and everything else imports through it.

## Migration from Radix UI

For developers migrating from **Radix UI**, many of our **UI primitives** include instructions and examples for translating equivalent Radix components to Base UI. This ensures a smooth transition while preserving accessibility, behaviors, and API patterns.

## Working with LLMs

We structure the documentation to make the components **AI-friendly**, so language models can understand, reason about, and modify them. To support this, we include:

- **[Agent Skills](/ui/docs/skills)** — Install coss knowledge directly into your AI assistant with `npx skills add cosscom/ui`. Covers all 53 primitives, styling conventions, migration patterns, and particle examples.
- A [llms.txt](/ui/llms.txt) file that provides a map of the documentation and component structure for your AI agent.
- A **Copy Markdown** button on every page, so you can easily share content or feed it to your AI workflows.
